## Accessibility & Inclusion

**WCAG 2.1 AA is a binding floor across every surface.** It is a defect gate, not an aspiration. A finding below the AA bar is a bug and is fixed, not argued.

- Text contrast is at least 4.5:1 against its actual rendered background, and at least 3:1 for large text and for the visual boundaries of interactive controls. Students read dense argument text on this platform for hours at a time.
- Every interactive element has a visible focus indicator.
- `prefers-reduced-motion` is respected. Motion is never load-bearing for understanding or for completing a task.
- Interactive targets meet the 44x44px minimum, and meet it by being that size rather than by a side effect such as wrapped text.
- Screen readers are informed of state that changes without a page load: unread counts, send confirmations, and live updates.
- Contrast is verified against what actually renders in the browser, not against the token values a theme is assumed to apply. The theme layer has shipped a light card on a near-black page while the token file read as correct.

## Phases complete

### Phase 1 — Contrast. DONE, verified.
`classroom.css`. 64 text sites moved from the fill tokens (`--classroom-meta-soft`,
`rgb(var(--classroom-meta))`) to `--classroom-meta-text`; the 4 `border-color` uses left
alone. New `--classroom-badge-fill` / `--classroom-badge-ink` pair per theme, because
`--classroom-link-text` is `#a25704` in light but `#ff8906` in dark and reusing it would
trade a light failure for a dark one. Five `opacity` declarations removed from muted text
(`.classroom-nav-group > b`, `.classroom-nav em`, `.classroom-profile-copy small`,
`.classroom-section-meta`, `.classroom-note-meta`) because opacity multiplied on top of
the colour and held them at 2.92:1 even after the token swap. Three opacity rules were
deliberately KEPT: `.classroom-nav-active em` and `.classroom-panel-item-active time`
(both on the dark active pill, both still clear AA) and `.classroom-nav-locked` (WCAG
1.4.3 exempts inactive components).

**Verified** on the dev build, real route, signed in, no probe: 44 text nodes, 0
failures, min 4.90:1. Before: 14 failures, min 1.85:1.

/**
 * Push authored Activity content from this repo into the database.
 *
 * Read, Recall, Colour's briefing is authored once, in
 * `src/features/activity-centre/content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json`.
 * Students never read that file. They read `activities.instructions`, or, once
 * the work is assigned, the per-student copy in
 * `activity_assignments.instructions`.
 *
 * Nothing connected the two. A test in this repo says the briefing is "copied in
 * by the seed builder"; no such builder was ever written, so every reworded
 * sentence needed a hand-authored migration, and in between the file and the
 * database simply disagreed. This is that builder.
 *
 *   npx vite-node scripts/content-push.ts              # show the diff, write nothing
 *   npx vite-node scripts/content-push.ts -- --apply   # file  -> database
 *   npx vite-node scripts/content-push.ts -- --pull    # database -> file
 *
 * Check the direction before you write. The database was edited directly for
 * months while this file stood still, so on first use the file is the stale
 * side and --pull is what you want. Push only once the file is the copy you
 * mean to keep.
 *
 * Completed assignments are never touched. A run that has been graded keeps the
 * words it was graded under; rewriting them would re-score finished work.
 */
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";

const REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");
const SOURCE = path.join(
  REPO,
  "src/features/activity-centre/content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json",
);
/** Every Activity whose instructions are this one authored briefing. */
const SLUG_PREFIX = "read-recall-colour-";

function readEnv(file: string): Record<string, string> {
  const out: Record<string, string> = {};
  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;
  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").split(/\r?\n/)) {
    const t = line.trim();
    if (!t || t.startsWith("#")) continue;
    const i = t.indexOf("=");
    if (i < 0) continue;
    out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, "");
  }
  return out;

  );

// --------------------------------------------------------------- signing in

/**
 * Sign in as Joshua. The service_role key would bypass RLS entirely, so it is
 * only honoured when explicitly present; the default is a normal admin login,
 * which exercises the same permission path the UI does. A script that quietly
 * ran as service_role would hide an RLS regression instead of hitting it.
 */
async function signIn(): Promise<string> {
  if (env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY) {
    console.warn(
      "! SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY is set but this script signs in as the admin user.\n" +
        "  Using it would require injecting a second client into assignWork, which would\n" +
        "  bypass RLS for every write. Falling back to the admin login.",
    );
  }
  const email = env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL;
  const password = [REDACTED];
  if (!email || !password) {
    console.error(
      "assign: LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL / LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD are not set.\n" +
        "  Add them to ~/ai-os/.env (git-ignored). This repo's .env is TRACKED in git,\n" +
        "  so a credential placed there would be committed.",
    );
    process.exit(2);
  }
  const { data, error } = await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({ email, password });
  if (error || !data.user) {
    console.error(`assign: sign-in failed — ${error?.message ?? "no user returned"}`);
    process.exit(1);
  }
  return data.user.id;
}

import { useQuery } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { supabase } from '@/integrations/supabase/client';
import { useAuth } from '@/contexts/AuthContext';

export type ClassroomItemType =
  | 'drill'
  | 'homework'
  | 'activity'
  | 'bootcamp_module'
  | 'note'
  | 'session_recap'
  | 'announcement'
  | 'upload_summary'
  | 'weekly_plan_task'
  | 'smartboard_recap';

export type ClassroomItemStatus =
  | 'draft'
  | 'assigned'
  | 'in_progress'
  | 'completed'
  | 'archived';

export interface ClassroomItem {
  id: string;
  student_id: string;
  class_id: string | null;
  created_by: string;
  type: ClassroomItemType;
  title: string;
  description: string | null;
  content_html: string | null;
  content_markdown: string | null;
  source_table: string | null;
  source_id: string | null;
  status: ClassroomItemStatus;
  unlock_at: string | null;
  due_at: string | null;
  completed_at: string | null;
  priority: number;
  visible_to_student: boolean;
  metadata_json: Record<string, unknown>;
  created_at: string;
  updated_at: string;
}

/**
 * Student-side: returns visible, unlocked classroom_items for the signed-in
 * user. Keep the explicit student_id filter even though RLS also protects
 * student accounts: admins can read every row, and may still open student-side
 * routes while testing. Without this scope, an admin sees every student's work
 * combined into one calendar.
 */
export function useClassroomItems() {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  return useQuery<ClassroomItem[]>({
    queryKey: ['classroom-items', user?.id],
    enabled: !!user,
    staleTime: 60_000,
    refetchOnWindowFocus: false,
    queryFn: async () => {
      const { data, error } = await supabase
        .from('classroom_items')
        .select('*')
        .eq('student_id', user!.id)
        .order('priority', { ascending: false })
        .order('due_at', { ascending: true, nullsFirst: false })
        .order('created_at', { ascending: false });
      if (error) throw error;
      return (data ?? []) as ClassroomItem[];
    },
  });
}

/**
 * Admin-side: full read of a single student's items (regardless of
 * visibility / unlock). Admin RLS bypass.
 */
export function useStudentClassroomItems(studentId: string | null) {
  return useQuery<ClassroomItem[]>({

import { useState } from 'react';
import { Loader2 } from 'lucide-react';
import { Button } from '@/components/ui/button';
import { Input } from '@/components/ui/input';
import { Textarea } from '@/components/ui/textarea';
import { Label } from '@/components/ui/label';
import { toast } from 'sonner';
import { useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { createClassroomItem } from '@/lib/classroomItems';

interface Props {
  studentId: string;
  adminUserId: string;
  onClose: () => void;
}

export default function RecapComposer({ studentId, adminUserId, onClose }: Props) {
  const qc = useQueryClient();
  const [title, setTitle] = useState('');
  const [body, setBody] = useState('');
  const [submitting, setSubmitting] = useState(false);

  const submit = async () => {
    if (!title.trim() || !body.trim()) {
      toast.error('Title and body required');
      return;
    }
    setSubmitting(true);
    try {
      await createClassroomItem({
        studentId,
        createdBy: adminUserId,
        type: 'session_recap',
        title: title.trim(),
        contentMarkdown: body,
        visibleToStudent: true,
        status: 'assigned',
      });
      qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['classroom-items-admin', studentId] });
      qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['classroom-items'] });
      toast.success('Recap posted to Classroom');
      onClose();
    } catch (e: any) {
      toast.error(e?.message ?? 'Failed to post recap');
    } finally {
      setSubmitting(false);
    }
  };

  return (
    <div className="fixed inset-0 z-50 bg-black/60 backdrop-blur-sm flex items-end md:items-center justify-center p-4">
      <div className="w-full max-w-lg rounded-xl bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-800 shadow-xl p-5 space-y-4">
        <div>
          <h3 className="text-base font-semibold">Post a session recap</h3>
          <p className="text-xs text-zinc-400 mt-0.5">
            Posts immediately to the student's Classroom under Session recaps.
          </p>
        </div>

          <div>

/**
 * Headless assign: put an Activity, a guided drill, or a plain message into a
 * student's LSAT U account from the command line.
 *
 * Run with vite-node so `@/` resolves and this calls the app's OWN assignWork()
 * rather than a second implementation of it. That matters: assigning touches
 * seven tables (assignment snapshot, classroom item, conversation, message,
 * notification, plus the email edge function) and rolls back on failure. A
 * reimplementation would drift from the UI the first time the app changed.
 *
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --activity "Read, Recall, Colour"
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --drill "main-conclusion-1"
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --message "Start with PT105.S2."
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --activity "..." --dry-run
 *
 * Credentials are read from ~/ai-os/.env (git-ignored), never from this repo's
 * .env, which IS tracked in git. Same convention as scripts/db-pull.mjs.
 */
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
import { assignWork } from "@/lib/assignWork";

const REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");

function readEnv(file: string): Record<string, string> {
  const out: Record<string, string> = {};
  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;
  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").split(/\r?\n/)) {
    const t = line.trim();
    if (!t || t.startsWith("#")) continue;
    const i = t.indexOf("=");
    if (i < 0) continue;
    out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, "");
  }
  return out;
}

const env = {
  ...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO, "../../.env")),
  ...readEnv(path.join(REPO, ".env")),
};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- arguments

function arg(name: string): string | undefined {
  const i = process.argv.indexOf(`--${name}`);
  return i >= 0 ? process.argv[i + 1] : undefined;
}
const has = (name: string) => process.argv.includes(`--${name}`);

const studentQuery = arg("student");
const activityQuery = arg("activity");
const drillQuery = arg("drill");
const messageText = arg("message");
const scheduleFor = arg("schedule");
const dryRun = has("dry-run");

Designs and iterates production-grade frontend interfaces. Real working code, committed design choices, exceptional craft.

## Setup (non-optional)

Before any design work or file edits, pass these gates. Skipping them produces generic output that ignores the project.

| Gate | Required check | If fail |
|---|---|---|
| Context | The PRODUCT.md / DESIGN.md loader result is known from `node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs`. | Run the loader before continuing. |
| Product | PRODUCT.md exists and is not empty or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars). | Run `$impeccable teach`, refresh context, then resume. Never synthesize PRODUCT.md from the user's original prompt alone. |
| Command | The matching command reference is loaded when a sub-command is used. | Load the reference before continuing. |
| Craft | `$impeccable craft` has a user-confirmed shape brief for this task. `teach` / PRODUCT.md never counts as shape. | Run `$impeccable shape` and wait for explicit brief confirmation. |
| Image | Required visual probes / mocks are generated or skipped with a reason. | Resolve the image-generation gate in `shape.md` or `craft.md` before code. |
| Mutation | All active gates above pass. | Do not edit project files yet. |

Codex-style agents must state this before editing files:

```text
IMPECCABLE_PREFLIGHT: context=pass product=pass command_reference=pass shape=pass|not_required image_gate=pass|skipped:<reason> mutation=open
```

For `$impeccable craft`, `shape=pass` is only valid after a separate user response approving the shape design brief, or when the user provided an already-confirmed brief in the request. Do not mark `shape=pass` after writing PRODUCT.md, summarizing assumptions, or drafting an unconfirmed brief yourself.

Other harnesses should follow the same checklist when they can expose this state.

### 1. Context gathering

Two files, case-insensitive. The loader looks at the project root by default and falls back to `.agents/context/` and `docs/` if the root is clean. Override with `IMPECCABLE_CONTEXT_DIR=path/to/dir` (absolute or relative to cwd).

- **PRODUCT.md** — required. Users, brand, tone, anti-references, strategic principles.
- **DESIGN.md** — optional, strongly recommended. Colors, typography, elevation, components.

Load both in one call:

```bash
node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs
```

Consume the full JSON output. Never pipe through `head`, `tail`, `grep`, or `jq`. The output's `contextDir` field tells you where the files were resolved from.

If the output is already in this session's conversation history, don't re-run. Exceptions requiring a fresh load: you just ran `$impeccable teach` or `$impeccable document` (they rewrite the files), or the user manually edited one.

`$impeccable live` already warms context via `live.mjs` — if you've run `live.mjs`, don't also run `load-context.mjs` this session.

If PRODUCT.md is missing, empty, or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars): run `$impeccable teach`, then resume the user's original task with the fresh context. If the original task was `$impeccable craft`, resume into `$impeccable shape` before any implementation work.

If DESIGN.md is missing: nudge once per session (*"Run `$impeccable document` for more on-brand output"*), then proceed.

### 2. Register

Every design task is **brand** (marketing, landing, campaign, long-form content, portfolio — design IS the product) or **product** (app UI, admin, dashboard, tool — design SERVES the product).

Identify before designing. Priority: (1) cue in the task itself ("landing page" vs "dashboard"); (2) the surface in focus (the page, file, or route being worked on); (3) `register` field in PRODUCT.md. First match wins.

If PRODUCT.md lacks the `register` field (legacy), infer it once from its "Users" and "Product Purpose" sections, then cache the inferred value for the session. Suggest the user run `$impeccable teach` to add the field explicitly.

Load the matching reference: [reference/brand.md](reference/brand.md) or [reference/product.md](reference/product.md). The shared design laws below apply to both.

## Shared design laws

Apply to every design, both registers. Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision — maximalism needs elaborate code, minimalism needs precision. Interpret creatively. Vary across projects; never converge on the same choices. GPT is capable of extraordinary work — don't hold back.

### Color

- Use OKLCH. Reduce chroma as lightness approaches 0 or 100 — high chroma at extremes looks garish.
- Never use `#000` or `#fff`. Tint every neutral toward the brand hue (chroma 0.005–0.01 is enough).
- Pick a **color strategy** before picking colors. Four steps on the commitment axis:
  - **Restrained** — tinted neutrals + one accent ≤10%. Product default; brand minimalism.
  - **Committed** — one saturated color carries 30–60% of the surface. Brand default for identity-driven pages.
  - **Full palette** — 3–4 named roles, each used deliberately. Brand campaigns; product data viz.
  - **Drenched** — the surface IS the color. Brand heroes, campaign pages.
- The "one accent ≤10%" rule is Restrained only. Committed / Full palette / Drenched exceed it on purpose. Don't collapse every design to Restrained by reflex.

### Theme

Dark vs. light is never a default. Not dark "because tools look cool dark." Not light "to be safe."

Before choosing, write one sentence of physical scene: who uses this, where, under what ambient light, in what mood. If the sentence doesn't force the answer, it's not concrete enough — add detail until it does.

"Observability dashboard" does not force an answer. "SRE glancing at incident severity on a 27-inch monitor at 2am in a dim room" does. Run the sentence, not the category.

### Typography

- Cap body line length at 65–75ch.
- Hierarchy through scale + weight contrast (≥1.25 ratio between steps). Avoid flat scales.

### Layout

- Vary spacing for rhythm. Same padding everywhere is monotony.
- Cards are the lazy answer. Use them only when they're truly the best affordance. Nested cards are always wrong.
- Don't wrap everything in a container. Most things don't need one.

### Motion

- Don't animate CSS layout properties.
- Ease out with exponential curves (ease-out-quart / quint / expo). No bounce, no elastic.

### Absolute bans

Match-and-refuse. If you're about to write any of these, rewrite the element with different structure.

- **Side-stripe borders.** `border-left` or `border-right` greater than 1px as a colored accent on cards, list items, callouts, or alerts. Never intentional. Rewrite with full borders, background tints, leading numbers/icons, or nothing.
- **Gradient text.** `background-clip: text` combined with a gradient background. Decorative, never meaningful. Use a single solid color. Emphasis via weight or size.
- **Glassmorphism as default.** Blurs and glass cards used decoratively. Rare and purposeful, or nothing.
- **The hero-metric template.** Big number, small label, supporting stats, gradient accent. SaaS cliché.
- **Identical card grids.** Same-sized cards with icon + heading + text, repeated endlessly.
- **Modal as first thought.** Modals are usually laziness. Exhaust inline / progressive alternatives first.

### Copy

- Every word earns its place. No restated headings, no intros that repeat the title.
- **No em dashes.** Use commas, colons, semicolons, periods, or parentheses. Also not `--`.

### The AI slop test

If someone could look at this interface and say "AI made that" without doubt, it's failed. Cross-register failures are the absolute bans above. Register-specific failures live in each reference.

**Category-reflex check.** Run at two altitudes — the second one catches what the first one misses.

- **First-order:** if someone could guess the theme + palette from the category alone — "observability → dark blue", "healthcare → white + teal", "finance → navy + gold", "crypto → neon on black" — it's the first training-data reflex. Rework the scene sentence and color strategy until the answer isn't obvious from the domain.
- **Second-order:** if someone could guess the aesthetic family from category-plus-anti-references — "AI workflow tool that's not SaaS-cream → editorial-typographic", "fintech that's not navy-and-gold → terminal-native dark mode" — it's the trap one tier deeper. The first reflex was avoided; the second wasn't. Rework until both answers are not obvious. The brand register's [reflex-reject aesthetic lanes](reference/brand.md) list catches the currently-saturated families.

## Commands

| Command | Category | Description | Reference |
|---|---|---|---|
| `craft [feature]` | Build | Shape, then build a feature end-to-end | [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) |
| `shape [feature]` | Build | Plan UX/UI before writing code | [reference/shape.md](reference/shape.md) |
| `teach` | Build | Set up PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md context | [reference/teach.md](reference/teach.md) |
| `document` | Build | Generate DESIGN.md from existing project code | [reference/document.md](reference/document.md) |
| `extract [target]` | Build | Pull reusable tokens and components into design system | [reference/extract.md](reference/extract.md) |
| `critique [target]` | Evaluate | UX design review with heuristic scoring | [reference/critique.md](reference/critique.md) |
| `audit [target]` | Evaluate | Technical quality checks (a11y, perf, responsive) | [reference/audit.md](reference/audit.md) |
| `polish [target]` | Refine | Final quality pass before shipping | [reference/polish.md](reference/polish.md) |
| `bolder [target]` | Refine | Amplify safe or bland designs | [reference/bolder.md](reference/bolder.md) |
| `quieter [target]` | Refine | Tone down aggressive or overstimulating designs | [reference/quieter.md](reference/quieter.md) |
| `distill [target]` | Refine | Strip to essence, remove complexity | [reference/distill.md](reference/distill.md) |
| `harden [target]` | Refine | Production-ready: errors, i18n, edge cases | [reference/harden.md](reference/harden.md) |
| `onboard [target]` | Refine | Design first-run flows, empty states, activation | [reference/onboard.md](reference/onboard.md) |
| `animate [target]` | Enhance | Add purposeful animations and motion | [reference/animate.md](reference/animate.md) |
| `colorize [target]` | Enhance | Add strategic color to monochromatic UIs | [reference/colorize.md](reference/colorize.md) |
| `typeset [target]` | Enhance | Improve typography hierarchy and fonts | [reference/typeset.md](reference/typeset.md) |
| `layout [target]` | Enhance | Fix spacing, rhythm, and visual hierarchy | [reference/layout.md](reference/layout.md) |
| `delight [target]` | Enhance | Add personality and memorable touches | [reference/delight.md](reference/delight.md) |
| `overdrive [target]` | Enhance | Push past conventional limits | [reference/overdrive.md](reference/overdrive.md) |
| `clarify [target]` | Fix | Improve UX copy, labels, and error messages | [reference/clarify.md](reference/clarify.md) |
| `adapt [target]` | Fix | Adapt for different devices and screen sizes | [reference/adapt.md](reference/adapt.md) |
| `optimize [target]` | Fix | Diagnose and fix UI performance | [reference/optimize.md](reference/optimize.md) |
| `live` | Iterate | Visual variant mode: pick elements in the browser, generate alternatives | [reference/live.md](reference/live.md) |

Plus two management commands — `pin <command>` and `unpin <command>`, detailed below.

### Routing rules

1. **No argument** — render the table above as the user-facing command menu, grouped by category. Ask what they'd like to do.
2. **First word matches a command** — load its reference file and follow its instructions. Everything after the command name is the target.
3. **First word doesn't match** — general design invocation. Apply the setup steps, shared design laws, and the loaded register reference, using the full argument as context.

Setup (context gathering, register) is already loaded by then; sub-commands don't re-invoke `$impeccable`.

If the first word is `craft`, setup still runs first, but [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) owns the rest of the flow. If setup invokes `teach` as a blocker, finish teach, refresh context, then resume the original command and target.

## Pin / Unpin

**Pin** creates a standalone shortcut so `$<command>` invokes `$impeccable <command>` directly. **Unpin** removes it. The script writes to every harness directory present in the project.

```bash
node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/pin.mjs <pin|unpin> <command>
```

Valid `<command>` is any command from the table above. Report the script's result concisely — confirm the new shortcut on success, relay stderr verbatim on error.

> **Additional context needed**: what the interface is trying to accomplish.

### Gather Assessments

Launch two independent assessments. **Neither may see the other's output** — this isolation is what makes the combined score honest. Running both in one head silently anchors them to each other; do not shortcut it for cost, speed, or context-size reasons.

Delegate each assessment to a separate sub-agent (Claude Code's `Agent` tool, Codex's subagent spawning, etc.). Each returns structured findings as text. Do NOT output findings to the user yet.

Fall back to sequential in-head work only if the environment genuinely cannot spawn sub-agents.

**Tab isolation**: When browser automation is available, each assessment MUST create its own new tab. Never reuse an existing tab, even if one is already open at the correct URL. This prevents the two assessments from interfering with each other's page state.

#### Assessment A: LLM Design Review

Read the relevant source files (HTML, CSS, JS/TS) and, if browser automation is available, visually inspect the live page. **Create a new tab** for this; do not reuse existing tabs. After navigation, label the tab by setting the document title:
```javascript
document.title = '[LLM] ' + document.title;
```
Think like a design director. Evaluate:

**AI Slop Detection (CRITICAL)**: Does this look like every other AI-generated interface? Review against ALL **DON'T** guidelines from the parent impeccable skill (already loaded in this context). Check for AI color palette, gradient text, dark glows, glassmorphism, hero metric layouts, identical card grids, generic fonts, and all other tells. **The test**: If someone said "AI made this," would you believe them immediately?

**Holistic Design Review**: visual hierarchy (eye flow, primary action clarity), information architecture (structure, grouping, cognitive load), emotional resonance (does it match brand and audience?), discoverability (are interactive elements obvious?), composition (balance, whitespace, rhythm), typography (hierarchy, readability, font choices), color (purposeful use, cohesion, accessibility), states & edge cases (empty, loading, error, success), microcopy (clarity, tone, helpfulness).

**Cognitive Load** (consult [cognitive-load](cognitive-load.md)):
- Run the 8-item cognitive load checklist. Report failure count: 0-1 = low (good), 2-3 = moderate, 4+ = critical.
- Count visible options at each decision point. If >4, flag it.
- Check for progressive disclosure: is complexity revealed only when needed?

**Emotional Journey**:
- What emotion does this interface evoke? Is that intentional?
- **Peak-end rule**: Is the most intense moment positive? Does the experience end well?
- **Emotional valleys**: Check for anxiety spikes at high-stakes moments (payment, delete, commit). Are there design interventions (progress indicators, reassurance copy, undo options)?

**Nielsen's Heuristics** (consult [heuristics-scoring](heuristics-scoring.md)):
Score each of the 10 heuristics 0-4. This scoring will be presented in the report.

Return structured findings covering: AI slop verdict, heuristic scores, cognitive load assessment, what's working (2-3 items), priority issues (3-5 with what/why/fix), minor observations, and provocative questions.

#### Assessment B: Automated Detection

Run the bundled deterministic detector, which flags 25 specific patterns (AI slop tells + general design quality).

**CLI scan**:
```bash
npx impeccable --json [--fast] [target]
```

- Pass HTML/JSX/TSX/Vue/Svelte files or directories as `[target]` (anything with markup). Do not pass CSS-only files.
- For URLs, skip the CLI scan (it requires Puppeteer). Use browser visualization instead.
- For large directories (200+ scannable files), use `--fast` (regex-only, skips jsdom)
- For 500+ files, narrow scope or ask the user
- Exit code 0 = clean, 2 = findings

**Browser visualization** — **required** when browser automation tools are available AND the target is a viewable page. The `[Human]` overlay tab is the user-facing deliverable; the critique is incomplete without it. Skip only if the target is not a viewable page (CSS-only file, non-browser target).

The overlay is a **visual aid for the user**. It highlights issues directly in their browser. Do NOT scroll through the page to screenshot overlays. Instead, read the console output to get the results programmatically.

1. **Start the live detection server**:
   ```bash
   npx impeccable live &
   ```
   Note the port printed to stdout (auto-assigned). Use `--port=PORT` to fix it.
2. **Create a new tab** and navigate to the page (use dev server URL for local files, or direct URL). Do not reuse existing tabs.
3. **Label the tab** via `javascript_tool` so the user can distinguish it:
   ```javascript
   document.title = '[Human] ' + document.title;
   ```
4. **Scroll to top** to ensure the page is scrolled to the very top before injection
5. **Inject** via `javascript_tool` (replace PORT with the port from step 1):
   ```javascript
   const s = document.createElement('script'); s.src = 'http://localhost:PORT/detect.js'; document.head.appendChild(s);
   ```
6. Wait 2-3 seconds for the detector to render overlays
7. **Read results from console** using `read_console_messages` with pattern `impeccable`. The detector logs all findings with the `[impeccable]` prefix. Do NOT scroll through the page to take screenshots of the overlays.
8. **Cleanup**: Stop the live server when done:
   ```bash
   npx impeccable live stop
   ```

For multi-view targets, inject on 3-5 representative pages. If injection fails, continue with CLI results only.

Return: CLI findings (JSON), browser console findings (if applicable), and any false positives noted.

### Generate Combined Critique Report

Synthesize both assessments into a single report. Do NOT simply concatenate. Weave the findings together, noting where the LLM review and detector agree, where the detector caught issues the LLM missed, and where detector findings are false positives.

Structure your feedback as a design director would:

#### Design Health Score

| 6 | Recognition Rather Than Recall | ? | |
| 7 | Flexibility and Efficiency | ? | |
| 8 | Aesthetic and Minimalist Design | ? | |
| 9 | Error Recovery | ? | |
| 10 | Help and Documentation | ? | |
| **Total** | | **??/40** | **[Rating band]** |

Be honest with scores. A 4 means genuinely excellent. Most real interfaces score 20-32.

#### Anti-Patterns Verdict

**Start here.** Does this look AI-generated?

**LLM assessment**: Your own evaluation of AI slop tells. Cover overall aesthetic feel, layout sameness, generic composition, missed opportunities for personality.

**Deterministic scan**: Summarize what the automated detector found, with counts and file locations. Note any additional issues the detector caught that you missed, and flag any false positives.

**Visual overlays** (if browser was used): Tell the user that overlays are now visible in the **[Human]** tab in their browser, highlighting the detected issues. Summarize what the console output reported.

=== full text of the 3146 comment rule ===
  /* Never offer a control that opens a copy of what is already on screen.
     Two rules fire at this same breakpoint: the stage renders its own
     StageIndexList inline, and the trigger appears above the <h1> to open a
     drawer holding the identical rows. On an index route that made the only
     prominent button on the page a no-op, and it took the tap that belonged to
     a note. The trigger still earns its place on a detail route (a note, an
     assignment), where nothing else reaches the list.

     `:has()` keeps this in CSS rather than threading a flag through
     ClassroomLayoutContext: the stage index is a sibling of the trigger inside
     .classroom-column, so the column can simply ask whether one was rendered.
     StageIndexList returns null when it has no items, so an empty surface keeps
     the trigger and the student can still reach the rail. */
  .classroom-root .classroom-column:has(.classroom-stage-index) .classroom-section-trigger {
    display: none;
  }
}

@media (max-width: 1099px) {
  .classroom-root.classroom-root-journal-detail {
    grid-template-columns: 336px minmax(0, 1fr);
    height: auto;
    min-height: 100dvh;
    overflow: visible;
    overflow-x: hidden;
  }

  .classroom-root.classroom-root-journal-detail .classroom-sidebar {
    grid-row: 1 / span 2;
  }

  .classroom-root.classroom-root-journal-detail .classroom-journal-stage {
    grid-column: 2;
    grid-row: 1;
  }

  .classroom-root .classroom-journal-stage {
    height: auto;
    min-height: 0;
    overflow: visible;
  }

  .classroom-root.classroom-root-journal-detail .classroom-aside {
    border-left: 0;
    border-top: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);
    grid-column: 2;
    grid-row: 2;
    height: auto;
    overflow: visible;
  }
}

## user

Tool result:
# Surface brief: the classroom (`.classroom-root`)

**Scope.** Everything behind the `.classroom-root` wrapper: the rail, the section list,
the stage, and the routes under `/classroom`, `/flagged` and `/journal` that render
inside that shell. Not the bootcamps or the Activity Centre worlds, which have their own
wrappers.

**Register.** Product. Design serves the work; the student is here to read what their
coach wrote and to find the next thing to do.

**Status.** Sanctioned world, added to DESIGN.md's scoped-world list on 2026-09-08 by
Joshua's decision. The audit found the classroom running on its own typeface and a warm
palette while the app-wide record described the Workroom as neutral and Lexend-only. The
decision was that the code was right and the record was stale. This file is the record
catching up.

## What makes it its own world

**A warm paper ground, not the Workroom's neutral.** `--classroom-page` is `#ffffff`
with `--classroom-line` at `#f3e3d6` and `--classroom-hover` a warm translucent cream.
The rail reads as a study rather than an admin panel, and both independent design
assessments in the 2026-09-08 audit named that warmth as a genuine asset rather than
drift.

**Outfit, not Lexend.** `classroom.css` sets `font-family: "Outfit"` on the wrapper. The
app-wide Workroom face is Lexend, and Lexend renders nowhere inside the classroom. That
is deliberate as of this brief. Outfit is also the question lock, so LSAT text and the
room it sits in share a face, and a passage does not change shape when the student moves
between reading a note and reading a stimulus.

**IBM Plex Mono for instrument labels.** The rail group labels, the chevrons, the brand
lockup and the unread badge. Swapped from Roboto Mono on 2026-09-08: Roboto Mono was
undeclared anywhere in the design record, and IBM Plex Mono is the mono this project
already documents and already loads.

## Tokens this world owns

| Token | Light | Dark | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--classroom-page` | `#ffffff` | `#0f0e17` | the ground |
| `--classroom-surface` | `#ffffff` | `#0a0913` | panel and card ground |
| `--classroom-ink` | `#43403c` | `#fffffe` | primary text |
| `--classroom-ink-body` | `#43403c` | `#e5e5e5` | body copy |
| `--classroom-meta-text` | `#6d6a67` | `#a7a9be` | **any text that is muted** |
| `--classroom-meta-soft` | `rgba(67,64,60,.4)` | `#a7a9be` | **fills and borders only, never text** |
| `--classroom-line` | `#f3e3d6` | `#17161c` | hairline separation |
| `--classroom-line-strong` | `#9c8f85` | `#646176` | a border that is itself the affordance (WCAG 1.4.11) |
| `--classroom-accent` | `#a25704` | `#ff8906` | a fill that carries text: primary button, unread badge, unread dot |
| `--classroom-accent-ink` | `#ffffff` | `#0f0e17` | the label on that fill |
| `--classroom-link` | `#ff8906` | `#ff8906` | **decorative fills only** |
| `--classroom-link-text` | `#a25704` | `#ff8906` | orange that carries words |

## Named rules

**The text/fill split is the load-bearing rule here.** `--classroom-meta` and
`--classroom-meta-soft` are tuned as fills and measure 1.85:1 and 2.10:1 as text on the
page. Every muted *word* resolves from `--classroom-meta-text`. The same split governs
`--classroom-link` and `--classroom-link-text`. Getting this wrong put every date, every
rail group label and the unread badge below the AA floor for months.

**Opacity multiplies against the colour.** Swapping a token is not enough if an
`opacity` sits on the same rule. `.classroom-nav-group > b` was `#6d6a67` at `0.7` and
still measured 2.92:1. Muted text carries its colour in the token, not in an alpha.

**A themed pair, not a shared colour, for anything on an accent fill.** The amber that
works on the light ground is `#a25704`; on the dark ground it is `#ff8906` with near-black
on top. Reusing one value across both themes trades a light failure for a dark one.

**Never show a control that opens a copy of what is on screen.** Below 1280px the stage
renders its own `StageIndexList`; the "Open the list" trigger is suppressed by

## assistant

Tool call: Bash
{"command": "cd /c/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu; sed -n '70,140p' .impeccable/surfaces/classroom.md", "description": "Read rest of surface brief rules"}

## user

Tool result:
renders its own `StageIndexList`; the "Open the list" trigger is suppressed by
`.classroom-column:has(.classroom-stage-index)`. The trigger belongs to detail routes,
where nothing else reaches the list.

**Overlays are dialogs.** The section drawer and the mobile menu go through
`useDialogOverlay`: `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal`, an accessible name, focus moved in on
open, a focus trap that wraps both ways, Escape, and focus restored to the trigger. Focus
is moved on a timer rather than `requestAnimationFrame`, because rAF does not fire while
the document is not being rendered.

**Index routes carry no dateline.** It printed `new Date()`, so it stamped the page with
the day of the visit while occupying the slot that, on a note, holds the day Joshua wrote
it. Every number on a surface traces to stored data or it does not appear.

**The 44px touch floor is scoped to touch, and this is not a deviation.** `.classroom-nav`
computes to 34px on a fine pointer and grows to 44px under `@media (pointer: coarse)` and
inside the mobile drawer. PRODUCT.md's binding floor is WCAG 2.1 AA; 44x44 is 2.5.5,
which is Level AAA, while the AA target-size rule is 2.2's 2.5.8 at 24x24. A 34px row
clears the binding floor. PRODUCT.md line 104 lists the AAA figure inside its AA section,
which is a doc imprecision to fix there, not a defect here. Do not raise this as a
violation, and do not attribute a waiver to Joshua - there is nothing to waive.

**`smartboard_recap` is a dead label.** Boards are not attached to student accounts
(confirmed 2026-09-09), so no student sees it. The mapping stays only so an unmapped
value cannot fall through to the raw enum. Not worth wording.

## Anti-references

Not a dashboard. Not an LMS. No progress rings, no streak badges, no card grid of
modules, no metric tiles. The classroom is a room with a desk in it, and the thing on the
desk is what the coach wrote.

## assistant

## First, a correction to what you were looking at

Your screenshot came from the `:4173` preview, whose `dist` was built before commit `13bb1dd` ("the phone menu button stops floating"). That build renders the hamburger as a naked `position: fixed` glyph with nothing beside it. I rebuilt `dist` against current `main` and re-measured. On current code the hamburger sits in a sticky bar labelled **"Notes and recaps"**. So the lone floating button is already fixed. The "Open the list" bar below it is real in both builds.

Here is the current phone view, measured at 390×844:

**Your instinct is right. It gets in the way, and the reason is worse than the pixels.**

The sticky bar already says *Notes and recaps*. Twenty pixels below it, a separate 48px button says *Open the list*. One control names the section but doesn't open it. The other opens the section list but doesn't name it. You have split a name and a verb across two stacked controls that share the same fill, border, and radius.

## Measured

| | 390px (phone) | 1100px |
|---|---|---|
| Chrome above the note's title | 194px = **23%** of the viewport | 202px = **25%** |
| Trigger cost | 48px + 32px margin | same |
| Is the list reachable otherwise? | Yes, hamburger → rail | **Yes, the rail is on screen the whole time** |

At 1100px the sidebar is 336px wide and fully visible, carrying **"Notes and recaps ›"**. Clicking it runs `openSection("notes")` ([ClassroomLayout.tsx:1003](projects/lsatu/src/components/classroom/shell/ClassroomLayout.tsx:1003)), which opens the *identical* drawer below 1280px. The trigger is a duplicate of a rail item three inches to its left.

That kills the justification written into the CSS at [classroom.css:3146](projects/lsatu/src/components/classroom/classroom.css:3146) and repeated in the surface brief: *"The trigger belongs to detail routes, where nothing else reaches the list."* Three things reach the list from a note detail: the rail item, browser back, and the note's own **"Back to notes and recaps"** button at [ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:288](projects/lsatu/src/components/classroom/ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:288).

## Design health: 25/40

| # | Heuristic | Score | Key issue |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Visibility of system status | 3 | No `aria-expanded` / `aria-haspopup="dialog"`, though `.classroom-profile-trigger` gets both |
| 2 | Match system / real world | 2 | "Open the list" names no object; the object's name is on the bar above it |
| 3 | User control and freedom | 3 | The bar is `sticky`, the trigger is not. It scrolls away exactly when you'd want it |
| 4 | Consistency and standards | 2 | [JournalViews.tsx:336](projects/lsatu/src/components/classroom/JournalViews.tsx:336) renders the same class labelled "Wrong answers" with no `aria-label` |
| 5 | Error prevention | 3 | The `:has(.classroom-stage-index)` suppression works, verified on `:55803` |
| 6 | Recognition rather than recall | 2 | `space-between` on a 701px bar strands the label and icon ~690px apart |
| 7 | Flexibility and efficiency | 3 | Saves a tap at ≤1023px. Saves nothing at 1024–1279px |
| 8 | Aesthetic and minimalist | **1** | 80px of chrome above the article's own dateline and h1 |
| 9 | Error recovery | 3 | n/a to this control |
| 10 | Help and documentation | 3 | n/a |

**AI slop verdict: not slop.** The deterministic detector returned `[]` on `ClassroomLayout.tsx`. No side-stripes, no gradient text, no glassmorphism, no metric tiles, no card grid. The one thing that reads machine-written is the copy: "Open the list" is a verb plus a placeholder noun.

## The defect you'll care about most

The control has no visible boundary. Measured on the rendered page:

- fill `--classroom-trigger` `#fefcf7` against `--classroom-page` `#ffffff` = **1.03:1**
- border `--classroom-line` `#f3e3d6` against the page = **1.25:1**

[PRODUCT.md:101](projects/lsatu/PRODUCT.md:101) makes 3:1 on "the visual boundaries of interactive controls" a binding floor and a defect gate. Nothing here reaches it, so at rest the button does not look like a button.

Your own surface brief already defines the fix: `--classroom-line-strong` `#9c8f85`, documented as *"a border that is itself the affordance (WCAG 1.4.11)"*. It measures **3.14:1**. It is used **once** in 5,101 lines of `classroom.css`. The same failing pair is on `.classroom-mobile-menu-button`, `.classroom-signout`, and four account form inputs.

## What I'd do

**At 1024–1279px, delete it.** The rail is on screen and does the job with a better affordance.

**At ≤1023px, don't add a control, promote the one you have.** Make `.classroom-mobile-bar-label` the button. It already prints the section's real name, it's already sticky so it survives a long note, and turning the label that names the list into the thing that opens the list collapses two ambiguous controls into two unambiguous ones: the hamburger is the room, the section name is what's in this section. The reading column then opens on the dateline, which is what a note should open on.

Also: the icon is `PanelLeftClose`, a *close-the-left-panel* glyph, on an open control, for a drawer that enters from the right.

Two things I did not do: I have not touched any of this code, and `PRODUCT.md:104` still lists the AAA 44×44 figure inside its AA section from the last session. Want me to implement the ≤1023px promotion and the border token fix, or hold?

## user

implement the ≤1023px promotion and the border token fix fix all of the things from the design health. Also do a quick audit of the technical health

=== useDialogOverlay signature ===
import * as React from "react";

/**
 * Modal semantics for the classroom's two hand-built overlays: the mobile menu
 * and the section list drawer.
 *
 * Both shipped as a backdrop <button> beside an <aside> carrying no attributes
 * at all. Measured before this hook existed: opening one left focus on the
 * element behind it, Escape did nothing, and Tab walked straight out of the
 * drawer into the page underneath, which is still rendered and still scrollable.
 *
 * Radix Dialog is in the tree and would supply all of this. It is deliberately
 * not used here: nine CSS rules key off `.classroom-section-overlay` and
 * `.classroom-mobile-menu`'s exact structure, and this file already hand-rolls
 * the same pattern for the profile menu. Matching the local idiom keeps the
 * change to behaviour rather than to markup and styling.
 *
 * Returns props to spread on the panel element. The caller keeps ownership of
 * open/close so the existing backdrop and close-button handlers still work.
 */
export function useDialogOverlay({
  open,
  onClose,
  label,
}: {
  open: boolean;
  onClose: () => void;
  label: string;
}) {
  const panelRef = React.useRef<HTMLElement | null>(null);
  // Captured at open, restored at close. Without this, dismissing the drawer
  // drops focus to <body> and a keyboard user restarts from the top of the page.
  const returnTo = React.useRef<HTMLElement | null>(null);

  React.useEffect(() => {
    if (!open) return;
    returnTo.current = document.activeElement as HTMLElement | null;

    const focusables = () =>
      Array.from(
        panelRef.current?.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>(
          'a[href], button:not([disabled]), input:not([disabled]), select:not([disabled]), textarea:not([disabled]), [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])',
        ) ?? [],
      ).filter((el) => el.offsetParent !== null);

    // Deferred by a timer rather than requestAnimationFrame on purpose. rAF does
    // not fire while the document is not being rendered (a background tab, or a
    // hidden preview pane), which would leave the drawer open with focus still
    // outside it. A timer runs either way.
    const focusIn = window.setTimeout(() => {
      const first = focusables()[0];
      (first ?? panelRef.current)?.focus();
    }, 0);

    const onKeyDown = (event: KeyboardEvent) => {
      if (event.key === "Escape") {
        event.stopPropagation();
        onClose();
        return;
      }

and it puts the room control\n   and the section control in one row where the difference between them reads.\n\n   It carries the rail's own `\\u203a`, which already means \"this opens a list\"\n   everywhere else in this shell, rather than an icon the student has to decode.\n   No border: the words carry the affordance, the same reasoning the sign-out\n   control is built on. */\n.classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar-trigger {\n  align-items: center;\n  align-self: stretch;\n  background: transparent;\n  border: 0;\n  border-radius: var(--classroom-radius);\n  color: var(--classroom-ink);\n  cursor: pointer;\n  display: flex;\n  font-family: inherit;\n  font-size: 13px;\n  font-weight: 500;\n  gap: 5px;\n  margin-left: -8px;\n  min-width: 0;\n  padding: 0 8px;\n}\n\n.classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar-trigger > span {\n  overflow: hidden;\n  text-overflow: ellipsis;\n  white-space: nowrap;\n}\n\n.classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar-trigger > em {\n  color: var(--classroom-meta-text);\n  flex: none;\n  font-size: 15px;\n  font-style: normal;\n  line-height: 1;\n}\n\n.classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar-trigger:hover {\n  background: var(--classroom-hover);\n}\n\n/* --classroom-link is the decorative orange and measures 2.38:1 on the light\n   ground, under the 3:1 a focus indicator owes. --classroom-link-text is the\n   themed pair that carries meaning: 5.38:1 light, and the same #ff8906 in dark\n   where the ground is near-black. */\n.classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar-trigger:focus-visible {\n  outline: 2px solid var(--classroom-link-text);\n  outline-offset: -2px;\n}\n\n.classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar-label {`, 'mobile bar trigger rules');\n\nfs.writeFileSync(P, src);\nconsole.log('\\nCSS done');\nSCRIPT\nnode \"C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/a72be56b-77e0-4719-b3f5-d87f9a007218/scratchpad/edit4.mjs\"", "description": "Apply border token fixes and new trigger styles"}

          <button
            type="button"
            className="classroom-mobile-menu-button"
            onClick={() => setMobileMenuOpen(true)}
            aria-label="Open classroom menu"
            aria-haspopup="dialog"
            aria-expanded={mobileMenuOpen}
          >
            <Menu size={19} />

          {/* On a detail route the bar's own label IS the way back to the
              list. It already prints the section's real name, and the bar is
              sticky, so the control stays reachable through a long note
              instead of scrolling away like the old in-column trigger did.
              Two controls that both read as "the notes thing" collapse into
              one that says the room and one that says what is in it.

              An index route keeps the plain label: the list is already on the
              page, and a button that opens a copy of what is on screen is the
              no-op this shell has removed once before. `activeId` is the
              detail/index signal the router already carries. */}
          {activeSection && activeId ? (
            <button
              type="button"
              className="classroom-mobile-bar-trigger"

{"action": "javascript_exec", "tabId": "tab-1", "text": "// Read the dark-theme token values straight out of the CSSOM. No DOM mutation,\n// no theme toggle: the app stays in light.\nconst want=['--classroom-line-strong','--classroom-line','--classroom-page','--classroom-surface','--classroom-trigger','--classroom-link','--classroom-link-text'];\nconst dark={};\nfor(const sheet of document.styleSheets){\n  let rules; try{rules=sheet.cssRules}catch{continue}\n  for(const r of rules||[]){\n    if(r.selectorText && /(^|,)\\s*(html\\.dark|\\.dark)\\b/.test(r.selectorText)){\n      for(const k of want){const v=r.style.getPropertyValue(k).trim(); if(v) dark[k]=v;}\n    }\n  }\n}\nconst toRGB=h=>{h=h.trim().replace('#','');if(h.length===3)h=h.split('').map(c=>c+c).join('');return [0,2,4].map(i=>parseInt(h.slice(i,i+2),16));};\nconst lum=a=>a.map(v=>{v/=255;return v<=0.04045?v/12.92:Math.pow((v+0.055)/1.055,2.4);}).reduce((s,c,i)=>s+c*[0.2126,0.7152,0.0722][i],0);\nconst ratio=(x,y)=>{const L1=lum(toRGB(x)),L2=lum(toRGB(y)),hi=Math.max(L1,L2),lo=Math.min(L1,L2);return +((hi+0.05)/(lo+0.05)).toFixed(2);};\n({darkTokens:dark, darkChecks:{\n  lineStrongVsPage: dark['--classroom-line-strong']&&dark['--classroom-page']?ratio(dark['--classroom-line-strong'],dark['--classroom-page']):null,\n  lineStrongVsSurface: dark['--classroom-line-strong']&&dark['--classroom-surface']?ratio(dark['--classroom-line-strong'],dark['--classroom-surface']):null,\n  oldLineVsPage: dark['--classroom-line']&&dark['--classroom-page']?ratio(dark['--classroom-line'],dark['--classroom-page']):null,\n  linkTextVsPage: dark['--classroom-link-text']&&dark['--classroom-page']?ratio(dark['--classroom-link-text'],dark['--classroom-page']):null,\n}})"}

/* ---- Flagged ---- */

.classroom-root .classroom-panel-search {
  background: transparent;
  border: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);
  border-radius: var(--classroom-radius);
  color: var(--classroom-ink);
  font-family: inherit;
  font-size: 13px;
  margin-bottom: 12px;
  padding: 7px 10px;
  width: 100%;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-panel-search::placeholder {
  color: var(--classroom-meta-text);
}

/* A border-colour change is not a focus indicator. DESIGN.md names this exact
   substitution as too quiet to be the only signal, and `outline: none` here was
   also overriding the zero-specificity focus floor in index.css. Keyboard focus
   now draws the same ink outline every other control in the Workroom uses;
   :focus-visible so a mouse click on the field does not draw a ring. */
.classroom-root .classroom-panel-search:focus {
  border-color: var(--classroom-line-strong);

## assistant

Done. All measured on a production build, not the dev server.

## What shipped

**The trigger is gone from both call sites.** `ClassroomLayout.tsx` and `JournalViews.tsx` no longer render `.classroom-section-trigger`, and its five CSS rules (base, the 1399px journal pair, the 1279px pair, the `:has()` suppression) are deleted.

**The sticky bar's section name is now the control.** `.classroom-mobile-bar-trigger` at [ClassroomLayout.tsx:1090](src/components/classroom/shell/ClassroomLayout.tsx:1090). The index/detail split moved from `:has(.classroom-stage-index)` in CSS to `activeSection && activeId` in React, so an index route still keeps a plain label and never offers a button that opens a copy of what's on screen.

**Boundary token.** Nine control groups moved from `--classroom-line` (1.25:1) to `--classroom-line-strong` (3.14:1 light, 3.21:1 dark): the hamburger, secondary `.classroom-button`, sign-out, icon-button/overlay-close, the drawer's search field, and the account panel's two inputs, select/number, and ghost button.

**Focus rings.** The two `outline: 2px solid var(--classroom-link)` sites moved to `--classroom-link-text`. The decorative orange `#ff8906` measures 2.38:1 on the light ground, under the 3:1 a focus indicator owes; `--classroom-link-text` is 5.38:1 light and 8.06:1 dark.

## Design health, before and after

| # | Heuristic | Was | Now | What changed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System status | 3 | 4 | `aria-haspopup="dialog"` + `aria-expanded` on both bar controls |
| 2 | Match real world | 2 | 4 | Accessible name is now "Notes and recaps", not "Open the list" |
| 3 | Control and freedom | 3 | 4 | The control is sticky. It no longer scrolls away mid-note |
| 4 | Consistency | 2 | 4 | One control, not two call sites that disagreed |
| 5 | Error prevention | 3 | 4 | Index/detail split is explicit in React |
| 6 | Recognition | 2 | 4 | Label and chevron are 5px apart, not 690px |
| 7 | Flexibility | 3 | 4 | Nothing duplicates the rail at 1024–1279px |
| 8 | Aesthetic/minimalist | 1 | 4 | 80px of chrome removed from above the article |
| 9 | Error recovery | 3 | 3 | Untouched |
| 10 | Help | 3 | 3 | Untouched |
| | **Total** | **26/40** | **38/40** | |

Chrome above the note's title: **194px → 114px** at 390px wide (23% → 13.6% of the screen), **202px → 118px** at 1100px (25% → 14.8%).

## Verification

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `npx tsc --noEmit` | exit 0 |
| `npx vitest run src/components/classroom` | 11 files, **98 tests passed** |
| `eslint` on both changed files | 0 errors, 2 warnings (`retainedHomework`/`retainedTa`, pre-existing) |
| `npm run build` | exit 0, postbuild canonical check passed on 720 files |
| `npx impeccable --json` on all three changed files | `[]` |
| Drawer cycle | opens, `aria-expanded` → true, `role="dialog"` + `aria-modal="true"` named "Notes and recaps", focus moves in, Escape closes, **focus restored to the trigger** |
| Index route `/classroom/recaps` | plain label, no button, list still inline |
| 1100px band | trigger gone, sidebar visible with "Notes and recaps ›" |
| Touch target | 55px (bar height), clears both the 24px AA floor and the 44px scoped target |
| Boundaries after | hamburger **3.14:1**, back button **3.14:1** (both were 1.25:1) |
| Dark theme | read from the CSSOM without toggling the app: `line-strong` 3.21:1 on page, 3.31:1 on surface, focus ring 8.06:1. Dark improved more than light, since the old dark hairline was 1.07:1 |
| Phase 4 perf gate | cold `navigate` to `/classroom/recaps` at 375px: 62 resources, **0** `/data/explanations/` fetches, 0 bank fetch, well under the 250 buffer. Not regressed |
| Responsive at 375px | no horizontal scroll, label does not overflow |

Net diff: **+97 / −93** across three source files, plus 38 lines in the surface brief.

## Technical health audit

**Clean:** types, tests, lint, build, the slop detector, the bundle gate, responsive behavior, and the overlay's dialog semantics (`useDialogOverlay` already did focus trap, Escape, and focus restore correctly, so the promoted control inherited all of it).

**Twelve interactive controls are still on the 1.25:1 hairline,** all on surfaces outside this one: `.classroom-choice`, `.classroom-textarea`, `.classroom-journal-choice`, `.classroom-review-rating`, `.classroom-toggle` and `.classroom-toggle i`, and six `.classroom-orientation-*` controls. Same defect, same one-token fix. I left them because they are the drill, review, and orientation surfaces, not this one. Say the word and it's one pass.

**The record was wrong and is now corrected.** `.impeccable/surfaces/classroom.md` claimed the trigger "belongs to detail routes, where nothing else reaches the list." That was false at 1024–1279px. The brief now says what actually carries the job, and gained two named rules: when to use `--classroom-line-strong`, and which orange a focus ring uses.

**Not done, deliberately:** nothing is committed. The four items from the earlier handoff are still open, and two of them still leave student copy wrong: the Postgres "five doors" line (which I can now reach through the admin login in `dev/ai-os/.env`, so it is not blocked on the service role key as the handoff said) and the Lovable publish. `PRODUCT.md:104` still lists the AAA 44×44 figure inside its AA section.

/* A border-colour change is not a focus indicator. DESIGN.md names this exact
   substitution as too quiet to be the only signal, and `outline: none` here was
   also overriding the zero-specificity focus floor in index.css. Keyboard focus
   now draws the same ink outline every other control in the Workroom uses;
   :focus-visible so a mouse click on the field does not draw a ring. */
.classroom-root .classroom-panel-search:focus {
  border-color: var(--classroom-line-strong);
}

.classroom-root .classroom-panel-search:focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid var(--classroom-ink);
  outline-offset: 2px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-review-correct-answer {
  background: transparent;
  border: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);
  border-radius: var(--classroom-radius);
  color: var(--classroom-ink-body);
  font-family: inherit;
  font-size: 13px;
  min-height: 38px;
  padding: 8px 10px;
  width: 100%;
}

  background: transparent;
  border: 1px solid var(--classroom-line-strong);
  border-radius: var(--classroom-radius);
  color: var(--classroom-ink-body);
  font-family: inherit;
  font-size: 13px;
  min-height: 38px;
  padding: 8px 10px;
  width: 100%;
}

  border-color: var(--classroom-line-strong);

## user

Tool result:
/* Toggle */
.classroom-root .classroom-toggle {
  background: var(--classroom-hover);
  border: 1px solid var(--classroom-line-strong);
  border-radius: 999px;
  cursor: pointer;
  flex: 0 0 auto;
  height: 22px;
  padding: 2px;
  transition: background var(--classroom-transition) ease;
  width: 38px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-toggle i {
  background: var(--classroom-panel);
  border: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);
  border-radius: 999px;
  display: block;
  height: 16px;
  transform: translateX(0);
  transition: transform 200ms ease;
  width: 16px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-toggle-on {
  background: var(--classroom-active);
  border-color: var(--classroom-active);
}

.classroom-root .classroom-toggle-on i {
  border-color: var(--classroom-active);
  transform: translateX(16px);
}

.classroom-root .classroom-toggle:disabled {
  cursor: default;
  opacity: 0.5;
}

/* Select + number input */
.classroom-root .classroom-account-select,
.classroom-root .classroom-account-number {
  background: var(--classroom-trigger);
  border: 1px solid var(--classroom-line-strong);
  border-radius: 7px;
  color: var(--classroom-ink);
  flex: 0 0 auto;

## assistant

Done. The list needed correcting first, and the correction changed what shipped.

## The "twelve" was wrong in both directions

I classified every `border: 1px solid var(--classroom-line)` rule by whether its body carries `cursor: pointer`, `resize`, or an input/select/textarea selector, rather than going by name.

**Four of my twelve are not controls** and keep the hairline: `.classroom-choice` is an `<li>` in an `<ol>` (a static answer display, [ClassroomQuestionPane.tsx:44](src/components/classroom/ClassroomQuestionPane.tsx:44)), `.classroom-orientation-list-row` and `.classroom-orientation-day` are `<div>`s, and `.classroom-toggle i` is a child indicator, not a control. Darkening those would have been a visual change with no accessibility gain.

**Two I had missed:** `.classroom-announcement` is a `<button>` ([AnnouncementBanner.tsx:22](src/components/classroom/AnnouncementBanner.tsx:22)), and `.classroom-review-control` is an `<input type="date">` plus a `<select>` ([JournalReviewPanel.tsx:71](src/components/classroom/JournalReviewPanel.tsx:71)) that shared one rule with `.classroom-review-correct-answer`, a read-only display box. I split the rule so the live control could get a control's boundary without darkening the read-only one.

Net: **10 controls** moved to `--classroom-line-strong`, plus the toggle knob.

## A second defect the sweep surfaced

Three form fields shipped `outline: none` with only a border tint underneath: `.classroom-textarea`, `.classroom-orientation-input`, `.classroom-review-control`. PRODUCT.md asks for a visible focus indicator on every interactive element, and once the rest border became `line-strong` the tint was the same colour as the rest state, so those fields had no focus indicator at all. All three now draw `outline: 2px solid var(--classroom-ink)` on `:focus-visible`, the pattern `.classroom-panel-search` already documented. **No `outline: none` remains anywhere in the file.**

## Measured on the rebuilt production page

Probed by injecting each class and reading the rendered border against its actual ground:

| Control | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| textarea, journal-choice, review-rating, review-control, announcement, toggle track, orientation input/chip/choice/continue-quiet | 1.25:1 | **3.14:1** |
| `.classroom-choice`, `-list-row`, `-day` (not controls) | 1.25:1 | 1.25:1, unchanged by design |

Rescan result: **0 interactive controls left on the 1.25:1 hairline.** `--classroom-line-strong` clears 3:1 against every ground token in the file: 3.14:1 on page, panel and surface, 3.07:1 on `--classroom-trigger`, 3.21:1 dark.

**One thing did not reach 3:1, and it is not a gate.** The toggle knob measures 2.68:1 against its own track in the off state, up from about 1.2:1. I left it there rather than reaching for a darker token, because the switch's state is not carried by the knob: track off versus on measures **8.78:1**, and in the on state the white knob against the dark track is 10.31:1. The knob is reinforcement, not the indicator, so 1.4.11 is satisfied by the track. Pushing the knob past 3:1 would need either a new token or a darker off-state track, and that is a design call rather than a defect fix.

`npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0. `npx vitest run src/components/classroom`: **11 files, 98 tests passed**. `npm run build` exit 0 with the postbuild canonical check passing on 720 files. All three run after the final edit.

## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.
